# How this repository was made (United States (Virginia))

This file is written by the build on 2026-11-10, the day the source data was retrieved.

## Sources

| Source | Address | Retrieved | Licence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Virginia Legislative Information System: Code of Virginia and Constitution of Virginia | <https://law.lis.virginia.gov/vacode/> | 2026-10-30 | Public statute text |
| Virginia Law Library: Code of Virginia, one CSV file per title | <https://law.lis.virginia.gov/law-library/> | 2026-11-02 | Public statute text; the site footer reserves rights in the site, not in the law. |
| Virginia Law Library, current municipal and county charters | <https://law.lis.virginia.gov/CSV/Charters.csv> | 2026-11-05 | Official Virginia statute text. |
| Virginia Law Library, authorities | <https://law.lis.virginia.gov/CSV/Authorities.csv> | 2026-11-10 | Official Virginia statute text. |
| Virginia Law Library, compacts | <https://law.lis.virginia.gov/CSV/Compacts.csv> | 2026-11-10 | Official Virginia statute text. |
| Virginia Law Library, Uncodified Acts of Assembly | <https://law.lis.virginia.gov/CSV/UnCodifiedActs.csv> | 2026-11-10 | Official Virginia statute text. |

Every word of law in this repository comes from these sources. Lexifina reads them and records each act's dated versions, and the laws that made each change, in one corpus file. This repository is built from that file.

## Dates

Each commit is dated at noon (America/New_York) on the day the change took effect. The other dates, enacted and published, are trailers.

When a law gives no date of its own, the default rule applies: No dated history is published: each text is the oldest version available, dated from the retrieval day. https://law.lis.virginia.gov/vacode/

## How the history is built

`tools/build.py` turns the corpus into this history in one pass: one commit per law, on the day it took effect. The same corpus always gives the same commit hashes. `tools/check_repo.py` reads every version back from git and compares it with the corpus. `tools/on.py` shows the law as it stood on any date. The corpus format is described in `tools/SCHEMA.md`.

- Corpus: sha256 `93e926a629346c492419084a6e3a430bb254fb707e6ed4c6f8cedcc4198d81e3` (91235848 bytes).
- Builder blob hashes (`git hash-object`): `build.py` `3024ff164c261807dfc4d5b7b1b4a2cde4357da1`, `check.py` `5d719354ea4c62d240c93d2cae446307d81d9e81`, `on.py` `aff48cf1b68d50c7ba8f399ca32261871c301824`.
- Python 3.12.10; git version 2.46.0.windows.1.

## Decisions made by hand

- English only. The Constitution and the Code of Virginia (every title).
- Every text has one version, "oldest version available", dated from the retrieval day and credited to the publisher; the site publishes only current text.
- Sections marked "Effective" on a date after 2026-10-30, or "Not in effect", are left out (2 such pages); "Effective until" versions are taken as current.
- The history line at the end of each section (the year and chapter citations) is not law text and is not kept.
- Wave 3 (2026-11-02): every Code of Virginia title is added from the Virginia Law Library CSV files, one act per title, each the oldest version available dated by the retrieval day (2026-11-02).
- A CSV row whose caption begins "(Effective until <date>)" or "(Repealed effective <date>)" is kept only if that date is after the retrieval day; "(Effective <date>)" only if the date is on or before it; of a contingent pair, the "expiration" text is kept and the "effective" one left out; "Not in effect" rows are left out. Sections shown as "Repealed", "Expired" or "Reserved" keep their one-line stub, as in the first build.
- The last paragraph of a CSV body is dropped when it is a history line (it begins with a year, "Acts <year>", "Code <year>" or "R. P. 1948"). Captions end with a full stop, as on the section pages.
- Each charter is one single-file act containing all normative text nodes from its current-charter row, in source order; identified editorial metadata is excluded.
- Current text is dated from its latest cited amendment or charter and Va. Code § 1-214(A), except where the official listing expressly gives a later effective day.
- Authorities and compacts (us-fill 2026-11-10): the uncodified ones in the Virginia Law Library files, one single-file act each; rows that are Code sections are in the Code titles already and are left out.
- An authority is dated from the latest creating act or amendment its row cites and Va. Code § 1-214(A) (as the charters); a compact from the latest passage or approval day its text gives.
- Uncodified Acts of Assembly (us-fill 2026-11-10): each act in the Virginia Law Library uncodified-acts file that carries its text is one act under uncodified-acts/, as enacted, credited to itself. Struck text (<s>) in amending acts is left out; inserted text is kept as plain text. Acts listed by title only are left out.
- In-force day of an uncodified act: the approval day when it carries an emergency clause; the day the act itself states for the whole act; otherwise the first day of July after a regular session (Va. Code § 1-214(A)); for a special session, the first day of the fourth month after the approval month (§ 1-214(B) counts from adjournment, which the file does not give). Later amendments are separate acts; no act is consolidated.

## What AI did and did not do

The scripts that read the sources and build this repository were written by Claude Code running Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) under Lexifina's direction.

No model wrote, translated or corrected legal text. No model runs at build time. Every word of law in this repository comes from the official sources above.

The commit messages are written by `build.py` from the source data.

Model use at data-build time:
- none (scripts only; no model wrote or altered legal text)
- A model designed the deterministic parser and reviewed source classifications; no model wrote or corrected law text.
- A model designed the deterministic parser; no model wrote or corrected law text.
- A model designed the deterministic parser; no model wrote or corrected law text.
